```json
{
  "server": "westd-45947",
  "command": "cd /root/colliforce-vla && python3 -c \"\nimport re\n\nwith open('scripts/eval_safelibero.py', 'r') as f:\n    content = f.read()\n\n# Add _merge_lora function after _inject_lora function (before SuffixOutputHook class)\nmerge_func = '''\n\ndef _merge_lora(model):\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Merge LoRA weights into base linear and replace LoRALinear with nn.Linear.\\\"\\\"\\\"\n    for module in model.modules():\n        for name, child in list(module.named_children()):\n            if isinstance(child, LoRALinear):\n                with torch.no_grad():\n                    delta = (child.scaling * (child.lora_A @ child.lora_B)).T\n                    child.base.weight.data += delta.to(child.base.weight.dtype)\n                setattr(module, name, child.base)\n\n\n'''\n\n# Insert before 'class SuffixOutputHook'\ncontent = content.replace('class SuffixOutputHook', merge_func + 'class SuffixOutputHook')\n\n# Add merge call after model.eval() in _load_policy_a3\ncontent = content.replace(\n    '    model.eval()\\n    return policy',\n    '    _merge_lora(model)\\n    logging.info(\\\"Merged LoRA weights into base model for torch.compile compatibility\\\")\\n    model.eval()\\n    return policy'\n)\n\nwith open('scripts/eval_safelibero.py', 'w') as f:\n    f.write(content)\n\nprint('Done')\n\""
}
```

_source line 868_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Another error! The `_merge_lora` function failed with:
```
RuntimeError: Expected all tensors to be on the same device, but found at least two devices, cuda:0 and cpu!
```

The issue is that after LoRA injection, the `lora_A` and `lora_B` parameters are loaded to `cuda:0` (from `torch.load(lora_path, map_location="cuda")`), but the base weights might be on a different device. Or the base weight is on `cuda:0` and the LoRA params are on CPU.


Actually, I think I see the real problem now. The `_load_policy_a3` function loads the base model first (which goes to cuda:0 by default), then injects LoRA where the parameters get created on CPU initially, and when the LoRA weights are loaded from the file with `map_location="cuda"` they end up on cuda:0, but then the merge operation tries to combine them with base weights that might still be on a different device.

The fix is to ensure everything gets moved to the same device before merging...

Writing merge function...
````

```json
{
```
```json
  "server": "westd-45947",
```
```json
  "command": "source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && cd /root/colliforce-vla && python -c \"\nwith open('scripts/eval_safelibero.py', 'r') as f:\n    content = f.read()\n\nold = '''def _merge_lora(model):\n    for module in model.modules():\n        for name, child in list(module.named_children()):\n            if isinstance(child, LoRALinear):\n                with torch.no_grad():\n                    delta = (child.scaling * (child.lora_A @ child.lora_B)).T\n                    child.base.weight.data += delta.to(child.base.weight.dtype)\n                setattr(module, name, child.base)'''\n\nnew = '''def _merge_lora(model):\n    for module in model.modules():\n        for name, child in list(module.named_children()):\n            if isinstance(child, LoRALinear):\n                with torch.no_grad():\n                    dev = child.base.weight.device\n                    lora_a = child.lora_A.to(dev)\n                    lora_b = child.lora_B.to(dev)\n                    delta = (child.scaling * (lora_a @ lora_b)).T\n                    child.base.weight.data += delta.to(child.base.weight.dtype)\n                setattr(module, name, child.base)'''\n\ncontent = content.replace(old, new)\n\nwith open('scripts/eval_safelibero.py', 'w') as f:\n    f.write(content)\nprint('Done')\n\""
}
```

````text
```
RuntimeError: Expected all tensors to be on the same device, but got mat2 is on cpu, different from other tensors on cuda:0 (when checking argument in method wrapper_CUDA_mm)
```

In `LoRALinear.forward()`:
```python
lora_out = (x.float() @ self.lora_A @ self.lora_B) * self.scaling
```

`x` is on `cuda:0` (input tensor from the model forward pass), but `self.lora_A` is on `cpu`. 

I see the issue now—the LoRA parameters are being loaded onto CUDA with `map_location="cuda"`, but then the `_inject_lora` function creates fresh Parameters on CPU in `LoRALinear.__init__` and copies the loaded values into them, which doesn't actually move them to the right device.

The problem is that `copy_()` preserves the destination tensor's device, so even though the source is on CUDA, the data gets copied back to CPU where the parameters were originally created. The fix is either to move the model to CUDA after loading the LoRA parameters, or to create the LoRA parameters on the same device as the base model from the start.

I need to check whether the policy handles device movement internally—the `infer` method might move tensors to the model's device, but the newly injected LoRA parameters won't follow that movement since they're nn.Parameters that weren't moved. The solution is to add device synchronization in `_load_policy_a3` after loading the LoRA parameters, moving them to match the model's device.
````

</details>

````text
STILL the same device mismatch error! `lora_A` is still on CPU. My device sync fix didn't work.

Let me think about why. The fix was:
```python
device = next(model.parameters()).device
for p in lora_params:
    p.data = p.data.to(device)
```

This should move the data of each LoRA parameter to the model's device. But `next(model.parameters()).device` — what if this returns `cpu`? The base model might be on CPU before the policy moves it to CUDA.

Let me check when the model gets moved to CUDA. 

The issue is that when `_load_policy_a3` creates the policy and injects LoRA, the model is still on CPU at that point. The policy's initialization or inference method might move it to CUDA later, but by then I've already tried to sync the LoRA parameters to the wrong device. I need to either move the LoRA params after the model is on its final device, or explicitly move them to CUDA upfront. I'm checking whether the LoRALinear modules are properly integrated into the model's module tree so that when `model.to(cuda)` is called, all parameters including the LoRA weights get moved along with it. The injection replaces the original linear layers with LoRALinear instances that register the base module and LoRA parameters, so they should move together when the model is relocated to a device.

But I'm realizing the actual issue: the policy moves the model to CUDA first, then LoRA injection happens afterward, which means the newly created LoRA parameters start on CPU and never get moved to match the model's device. The cleanest solution is to iterate through all LoRALinear modules after loading and explicitly move them to CUDA, rather than trying to infer the device from existing parameters. Let me implement this fix and restart the process.
````

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Eval 3a exited (exit=0) very quickly again — the EGL errors are cleanup/destruction errors from robosuite. But did it actually crash or complete? The fact that it says "exit=0" suggests it completed... but the script uses `2>&1 | tee`, so the exit code reported is from `tee`, not from the Python script. With `set -e`, the bash script would only continue if the pipeline succeeded. But with pipes, `set -e` tracks the last command in the pipeline (tee), which always exits 0.

Wait, actually `set -e` with pipe: the default behavior in bash is that the pipeline's exit status is the exit status of the last command. 

So the Python script could've crashed but the runner script kept going since tee always exits cleanly. I need to check the refine.log to see what actually happened.
```
